# Project Progress — Saha Textile (cross-tool checkpoint)

> **This file is a living checkpoint for the entire project until launch (and post-launch hardening).**
> It exists so progress is **not trapped inside one chat, one IDE, or one agent**. Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, parallel subagents, and humans all **read it before starting work** and **update it after finishing each small task**.

---

## Why this file exists (read this every session)

| Problem                                                               | How this file solves it                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| Switching from Cursor → Claude Code → Codex loses conversation memory | Progress lives in git, not in a chat transcript             |
| Parallel agents collide or redo finished work                         | Each agent checks the matching section first                |
| “What is done vs still scaffolding?” is unclear                       | Bullets are dated, tool-attributed, and status-tagged       |
| New agent invents a different plan                                    | Roadmap + this file + owner log are the shared ground truth |

**Non-negotiable maintenance rules (all tools):**

1. **Before any task:** read this file’s matching section(s) + `api-db-development-roadmap-with-pending-decision-gates.mdx` § “Required reading / context pack”.
2. **After each small completed task:** append a bullet under the right heading (do not rewrite history; append).
3. **Bullet format (required):**
    - `- YYYY-MM-DD — [STATUS] short fact. Tool: <cursor|claude-code|codex|human>. Notes: optional.`
    - `STATUS` ∈ `DONE` | `PARTIAL` | `BLOCKED` | `REVERTED` | `N/A`
4. **Never delete** prior bullets unless they were factually wrong — then mark `REVERTED` and add a correcting bullet.
5. **Do not store secrets** here (no passwords, tokens, connection strings with passwords, Working Keys).
6. **Commit this file** with the same PR/commit set as the work it records whenever practical.
7. **Ignore** `project-context/nextjs-context/` — superseded stack; never log Next work as current progress.
8. If two tools finished related work: both append; if conflict, prefer **code + owner-decisions-log** over a stale bullet, and append a reconciliation note.

**Related planning docs (do not replace this file):**

- Build order / gates: `api-db-development-roadmap-with-pending-decision-gates.mdx`
- Local env handoff: `api-db-local-environment-bootstrap.mdx`
- Locks: `owner-decisions-log.mdx`
- Constitution: `AGENTS.md` (repo root)

---

## How to use when starting a new chat / tool

Paste or `@`-attach at minimum:

1. This file (`project-progress.mdx`)
2. `api-db-development-roadmap-with-pending-decision-gates.mdx`
3. `owner-decisions-log.mdx`
4. `AGENTS.md`

Then say which chunk/section you are working (e.g. “Chunk C — Mongo + env sync”).

---

## Legend

| Tag        | Meaning                                                  |
| ---------- | -------------------------------------------------------- |
| `DONE`     | Shipped in repo and verified enough for the stated scope |
| `PARTIAL`  | Started / scaffolded; not DoD                            |
| `BLOCKED`  | Waiting on owner gate, credential, or dependency         |
| `REVERTED` | Prior claim undone                                       |
| `N/A`      | Not in scope for current phase                           |

---

## Machine-readable portal truth snapshot

> **Governed derived state for portal instruments.** The build-time portal-data compiler reads this JSON block; do not rename its markers. Update the snapshot in the same change as the dated progress bullet or owner-gate decision it reflects. The compiler cross-checks chunks against the roadmap/progress evidence and gates against the roadmap plus owner log.

{/* portal-truth:start */}

```json
{
	"schemaVersion": 1,
	"lastVerified": "2026-08-18",

		"A": "done",
		"B": "done",
		"C": "done",
		"D": "partial",
		"E": "done",
		"F": "planned",
		"G": "planned",

		"I": "planned",
		"J": "planned"
	},
	"gates": {
		"G-NUM-ORDER": "open",
		"G-NUM-PI": "open",
		"G-CORE-CONTRACTS": "resolved",
		"G-I18N-ROUTES": "open",
		"G-IMG-PX": "open"
	}
}
```

- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk C Pass 1 (API platform: request id, redacted logging, error envelope, proxy/CORS, health split).**
  (1) **Correlation id** on every request via Fastify `genReqId`, echoed back on every
  response through an `onSend` hook. An inbound `x-request-id` is honoured ONLY when
  `TRUST_PROXY` is on and it matches a strict charset/length pattern — otherwise any
  client could collide ids or inject newlines into the log stream. (2) **Redacted
  pino logging** at the adapter (`authorization`, `cookie`, `x-csrf-token`,
  `set-cookie`, and body `password`/`newPassword`/`currentPassword`/`pin`/`code`/
  `token`/`csrfToken`), so no call site can forget; level is config-driven and silent
  under test. (3) **Global `HttpExceptionFilter`** maps every failure to the
  `ApiErrorResponse` envelope added in Pass 1b, with a SAFE per-status message rather
  than the exception text — a Mongo/driver/provider string can no longer escape — and
  logs the real cause server-side against the same `requestId` the client receives.
  Validation issues pass through deliberately (they describe the caller's own input).
  (4) **`resolveClientIp`** reads the configured header only when the proxy chain is
  trusted, and now KEYS THE RATE LIMITER — previously every request behind a proxy
  shared the proxy's address, so one caller could exhaust the limit for everyone.
  (5) **Strict CORS** with explicit methods/allowed/exposed headers alongside the
  existing exact-origin allowlist and credentials. (6) **`/health/live` +
  `/health/ready` split**: liveness is deliberately dependency-free (a Mongo blip must
  not make the orchestrator crash-loop healthy containers); readiness reports each
  dependency and fails closed with 503. `/health` kept as an alias.
  **Two latent problems found and fixed:** `fastify` was never a declared dependency
  of `apps/api` despite its types being imported (only transitive — pnpm's strict
  layout surfaced it once tests were typechecked); and `apps/api` had no typecheck
  config covering `test/`, so a new `tsconfig.typecheck.json` was added and
  mutation-verified like the other two packages. **Live probe against a fresh build:**
  `/health/live` 200 with a unique `x-request-id` per request; a spoofed inbound
  `x-request-id` discarded (`TRUST_PROXY` off); `/health/ready` 200 with
  `mongodb: up`; after `pnpm mongo:down`, `/health/ready` → **503 `readyState=0`**
  while `/health/live` stayed **200**; a 404 returned
  `{"error":{"code":"not_found","message":"The requested resource was not found.","issues":[],"requestId":"…"}}`
  with no internal detail. Mongo restored to rs0 PRIMARY afterwards. New
  `apps/api/test/platform.test.ts` = 13 tests. Gates: typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15,
  `pnpm test` 9/9, naming, format green. Remaining in Chunk C: cookie/CSRF
  foundation, rate-limit tuning per route class, and the Mongo `TransactionManagerPort`
  adapter with commit/rollback tests against rs0. Tool: claude-code.

  `isSearchIndexable`, `resolveProductStatusFilter`, `discontinuationPurgeDueAt`,
  `isDiscontinuationPurgeDue`), so changing it is config, not a migration.
  **Security fix in the same pass:** the public catalog previously applied a status
  clause only when the caller supplied one, and `getProduct` applied none at all —
  so `GET /catalog/products` returned drafts and disabled products, and any draft
  was readable by slug. `ProductFilter`/`ProductRepository` now carry a
  `CatalogAudience` that defaults to `public` (fails closed), the Mongo repository
  resolves the allowed status set centrally (a public caller asking for `draft`
  gets `$in: []`, never a widened result), the public controller no longer accepts
  a `status` query param at all, and a hidden product is a 404 rather than an
  "exists but hidden" signal. Mongo model enum + `lifecycle` sub-document + two new
  indexes (`status+categoryIds+createdAt`, `status+lifecycle.discontinuedAt` for the
  retention job); mapper fills lifecycle defaults; seed `published` → `live`.
  **Verified:** repo typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15 (0 errors), check:naming, format:check;
  contracts 68/68, core-domain 30/30; `pnpm mongo:up` (rs0 PRIMARY) + `RUN_DB_IT=1`
  adapter integration 7/7 including new cases proving draft/disabled/discontinued
  products are invisible to public `list`/`findById`/`findBySlug`, cannot be widened
  by an explicit status, and ARE visible to `audience: 'admin'`. Live API probe
  against a fresh build with 5 products (3 live, 1 draft, 1 disabled): public list
  returned 3 live, `?status=draft` still returned the 3 live, `/catalog/products/probe-draft`
  and `probe-disabled` returned 404, live slug returned 200; probe fixtures removed
  afterwards (DB back to 3 live). Tool: claude-code. Notes: `apps/api`'s `pnpm start`
  is `node dist/main.js` — an initial probe measured a 2026-07-26 `dist` and was
  discarded; always `pnpm build` before probing the API.

- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk D complete (sessions, OTP, PIN, RBAC, ownership, privacy).**
  The transitional bearer-JWT auth is GONE; the browser happy path is cookie-only.
  **Sessions:** `SessionService` is the single place a session is born or rotated —
  opaque 256-bit refresh tokens (never JWTs), HMAC-peppered so a database dump cannot
  verify guesses offline, storefront idle 30d/absolute 90d vs admin idle 60m/absolute
  12h (soft-lock UI 15m; server idle must outlive it). Reuse detection is real: presenting a rotated-away token, or losing the rotation
  race, revokes the whole `refreshFamilyId`. Every refresh re-reads the user, so a
  disabled account cannot renew itself. **Guards:** `SessionGuard` is global — routes are
  protected by DEFAULT and opt out with `@Public()`; it reads the access token from the
  httpOnly cookie only, and compares the token's `tokenVersion`/`permissionsVersion`
  against the user's current values so a password or role change invalidates immediately.
  `@Audience('storefront'|'admin')` enforces the locked isolation. `assertOwnership()`
  throws **404, not 403**, so an id cannot be probed for existence. **Endpoints:**
  storefront register/login/OTP-request/OTP-verify/refresh/logout/forgot/reset/me and
  admin login/PIN-login/PIN-setup/refresh/logout/me, plus privacy consent read/write and
  export/erase seams. OTP is ours end to end (CSPRNG, HMAC-at-rest, single active
  challenge, attempt-capped, atomic consume) — no provider widget. Anti-enumeration is
  uniform: unknown address, wrong password and inactive account all return the same 401,
  a dummy argon2 verify equalises timing, and forgot-password/OTP-request always return
  the same accepted message. A password reset bumps `tokenVersion` AND revokes every
  session. `ConsoleNotificationAdapter` satisfies `NotificationPort` and logs only a
  hashed destination with the payload withheld — OTP codes and reset tokens travel in
  `variables` and must never reach a log.
  **Three real defects found and fixed during live verification:**
  (1) **BOLA:** `GET /orders/:id` had NO ownership check — any authenticated customer
  could read any order by id. Now ownership-checked with a 404 for non-owners and a
  staff/admin support bypass. (2) **CSRF rotation:** `refresh()` issued a new CSRF token
  but never updated the stored `csrfSecretHash`, so every state-changing request after a
  refresh would 403 forever; the secret now rotates in the SAME atomic update as the
  refresh token, and the port documents why. (3) **Sparse-index trap:** `username`/`phone`
  used sparse unique indexes, but sparse still indexes an explicit `null`, so the SECOND
  customer without a username collided (`E11000 username_1 dup key: null`). Switched to
  partial indexes filtered on `$type: 'string'`; local indexes dropped and rebuilt.
  **Live proof on a fresh build:** register 201 with cookies and NO tokens in the body;
  `/auth/storefront/me` 200; `/auth/admin/me` **401 with a storefront session** (audience
  isolation); `/orders` 401 without a session and 200 with one; refresh rotates the token;
  replaying the old token → 401 plus the logged family revocation; a POST after refresh →
  204 (the fixed CSRF bug); two usernameless registrations both 201 (the fixed index bug);
  wrong password, unknown email and inactive all → 401; forgot-password byte-identical for
  known and unknown addresses; and a log scan found zero OTP codes or tokens. Gates:
  typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15, `pnpm test` 9/9, naming, format, DB IT 31/31 green.
  Deferred with reasons: Google/Facebook token verification (needs approved provider
  credentials — `OAuthStateRepository` and the state collection are in place), admin
  invite ACCEPTANCE endpoint (repository + collection exist; the flow belongs with admin
  user management), and idle quick-resume UX. Tool: claude-code.
- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk D route gaps closed (email verification, admin invites, quick-resume).**
  Reconciled against the Cursor/Codex fixes first, then closed the remaining Chunk D
  boundary items that had services but NO routes — an email-verification token was being
  issued at registration with no endpoint to redeem it, and the admin invite contract,
  collection and repository all existed while there was **no way to create an admin
  account at all**. Added: `POST /auth/storefront/email/verify` (public; single-use,
  atomic consume; expired/used/unknown all answer identically) and
  `POST /auth/storefront/email/verify/resend` (session-REQUIRED on purpose — an
  unauthenticated resend taking an arbitrary address is both an enumeration oracle and a
  way to have us mail strangers; already-verified and rate-limited callers get the same
  accepted response). `POST/GET/DELETE /auth/admin/invites` (admin-role only) and
  `POST /auth/admin/invites/accept` (public, because the invitee has no session — the
  single-use token IS the authorization). `POST /auth/admin/resume` implements the locked
  idle quick-resume: it re-verifies the PIN for the CURRENT session's own user and
  extends that session rather than issuing a new one, which is what lets the admin UI keep
  its mounted route/form state. New `AdminInviteService` writes the invite and its audit
  row in ONE transaction (a granted privilege must never exist without evidence of who
  granted it) under the seven-year `financial_security` tier, and bound
  `AUDIT_LOG_REPOSITORY` in DI — it was implemented in Chunk E but never wired, so no
  admin mutation had been auditable until now. **Live-verified on a fresh build:** all
  seven routes mapped; invalid verification token → 401; resend 401 without a session and
  202 with; invite creation 401 for anonymous AND for a customer session, 201 for an
  admin; the 201 response and the list response contain NO token material; DB check
  confirms only a 64-char `tokenHash` is stored and the audit row is
  `admin.invite.create` / `financial_security` / `warn` with no token material; and
  `invites/accept` with a malformed body returns 400 `validation_failed`, proving the
  route is genuinely public rather than being rejected by the guard. Probe data cleaned
  up afterwards. 8 new unit tests (api 57 → 64). Gates: typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15,
  `pnpm test` 9/9, naming, format, DB IT 81/81. Remaining in D: OAuth provider
  verification only, which is blocked on approved credentials. Tool: claude-code.
- 2026-08-01 — [PARTIAL] **Chunk D completion label corrected after portal/code reassessment.** The delivered cookie session, rotation/reuse, session-bound CSRF, audience, OTP/password-reset, PIN/RBAC/version invalidation, consent/privacy and order-ownership mechanics are real. The full roadmap boundary is not complete: no email-verification completion route, OAuth verification, admin invite acceptance or quick-resume exists, and `CartController` remains `@Public()` with arbitrary cart ids and no guest proof/user ownership (explicitly deferred there to Chunk G). The governed `portal-truth` snapshot correctly remains D=`partial`; the roadmap now records D2 done and D3–D5 partial. Tool: codex.
- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk E COMPLETE (passes 2–4: inventory, media, governance, content).**
  **Inventory:** `applyDelta` moves stock with a single conditional `$inc` and the oversell
  guard INSIDE the query, so neither the update nor the check can be raced. Proven under
  real concurrency: ten parallel decrements observe ten DISTINCT balances (a
  read-then-write implementation repeats values), and eight concurrent buyers against
  three units produce exactly three successes with stock landing at zero rather than
  negative. FIFO consume draws oldest-first with a conditional decrement per layer and
  REFUSES to cost more units than were received (silently costing the remainder at zero
  would corrupt margin reporting); release reverses onto the same layers; both roll back
  with their transaction. **Media:** assets are reference-counted across every owning
  surface (product galleries, variant primaries, attribute swatches) so orphan collection
  cannot delete bytes another owner still renders; soft delete plus a grace cutoff drives
  the GC sweep; a partial unique index on the storage key allows many purged-master rows
  while still preventing key reuse. **Governance:** `auditLogs` is append-only with no
  update or delete path, and `append` joins the caller's transaction so a mutation and its
  evidence commit together — tested by failing the transaction and asserting no orphan
  audit row survives; retention purges one tier without touching the other. Notification
  settings are unique per channel × category with atomic usage counting, and the outbox
  enforces idempotency through a partial unique index so a retried OTP or order
  confirmation returns the recorded result instead of sending twice. **Content:** FAQ
  resolution dedupes a `mixed`-scope entry that matches through both its category and
  product target; `askerEmail` is `select: false` and the public Q&A path never selects it
  (not merely hides it downstream); reviews are unique per (product × user × order), and
  `recomputeAggregate` reads APPROVED reviews only and runs in the same transaction as the
  moderation decision, so the aggregate can never disagree with the reviews behind it.
  **Mapper response-safety tests** (an explicit Chunk E DoD item): `toUser` is loaded with
  `+passwordHash +pinHash` selected — the worst case — and the serialized result is
  asserted to contain no hash, no `tokenVersion`/`permissionsVersion`, and to still parse
  as the public `User` contract; `toProduct` is asserted to emit no `_id`/`__v`.
  Adapter tests 46 → **77**. Gates: typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15, `pnpm test` 9/9, naming,
  format, DB IT 77/77 green. Chunk E Definition of Done met: model/index tests cover
  uniqueness, TTL, public visibility and ownership lookups; transaction rollback tests
  prove atomic workflows; mapper tests prove response-safe shapes. Tool: claude-code.
- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk E Pass 1 (catalog persistence: structure, variants, merchandising).**
  Six new collections behind the Chunk B ports: `categoryPlacements`,
  `categoryFacetConfigs`, `attributeDefinitions`, `productVariants`, `productBundles`,
  `productRelations`, plus their repositories. Public reads reuse
  `resolveProductStatusFilter`, so placements/variants/relations fail CLOSED to `live`
  exactly like products. `moveSubtree` recomputes every descendant path inside the
  caller's transaction and refuses a move that would make a placement its own ancestor;
  `saveMany` writes a whole variant matrix atomically so a product is never left
  half-migrated between axis sets; `replaceFromInsightSet` swaps a weekly analytics run
  as a unit so a rail never renders a half-updated mix; `isBundleProduct` is deliberately
  status-agnostic because a DRAFT bundle is still a bundle for `DEC-BUNDLE-NESTING`.
  **A real index bug was caught by the new tests:** `{ pathSlugs: 1 }` unique on an ARRAY
  is a MULTIKEY index — uniqueness applies per ELEMENT, so `['sarees']` and
  `['sarees','pure-silk']` collided on the shared segment and no category could ever have
  a child (`E11000 pathSlugs_1 dup key: "root"`). Replaced with a derived scalar
  `pathKey` (`pathSlugs.join('/')`, computed on write so it cannot drift) carrying the
  unique index; the stale index was dropped locally. Other indexes with explicit purpose:
  partial-unique canonical placement per category, unique `(productId,
optionSelectionHash)`, globally unique variant SKU per `DEC-SKU`, unique bundle per
  product, and unique `(source, type, target)` relation. New
  `test/catalog-persistence.test.ts` = 15 tests (adapter total 31 → 46) covering canonical
  uniqueness, path uniqueness, public-visibility hiding, transactional subtree move,
  cycle refusal, duplicate-combination and duplicate-SKU rejection, purchasable filtering,
  atomic matrix rollback, the nesting guard on a draft bundle, relation auto-pause,
  insight-set replacement, scheduling windows, and attribute-code uniqueness. Gates:
  typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15, `pnpm test` 9/9, naming, format, DB IT 46/46. Tool: claude-code.
- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk D Pass 1 (auth persistence: models, repositories, TTL policy).**
  Six new collections behind the Pass-4 ports: `authSessions`, `otpChallenges`,
  `oauthStates`, `passwordResetTokens`, `emailVerificationTokens`, `adminInvites`,
  `authRateLimits`. Every credential-shaped field (`refreshTokenHash`,
  `previousRefreshTokenHash`, `csrfSecretHash`, `codeHash`, every `tokenHash`,
  `passwordHash`, `pinHash`) is `select: false`, so an unqualified `find()` cannot leak
  it — asserted by test, not just by convention. `users` gained the auth fields the
  contracts already defined: status, phone/phoneVerified, username, pinHash,
  preferredLoginMethod, permissions, tokenVersion, permissionsVersion, failed-attempt
  counters, pinLockedUntil, lastLoginAt, adminProfile, plus sparse-unique username/phone
  indexes. **Concurrency-safe by construction:** rotation is one conditional update
  (`_id` + current hash + not revoked) so a replayed refresh loses; OTP consume and
  attempt counting are atomic; a partial unique index enforces ONE active challenge per
  (identifier × purpose); single-use tokens consume via `findOneAndUpdate`. TTL indexes
  on every transient collection, and deliberately NONE on `adminInvites` — an accepted
  invite is admin-account audit evidence under the 7-year tier, not transient state.
  **A real bug was caught by the new tests:** the rate-limit counter used a plain upsert,
  and concurrent hits that all miss the row race to insert it — MongoDB raises E11000 for
  the losers rather than converting them to updates. Five simultaneous login attempts
  would have surfaced as 500s at exactly the moment the limiter is supposed to work.
  Fixed with a duplicate-key retry (the row exists by then, so the retry takes the update
  path); the test asserts five parallel hits observe five DISTINCT counts, which a
  lost-update implementation cannot satisfy. New `test/auth-persistence.test.ts` = 18
  tests against rs0 (adapter total 13 → 31): hash invisibility, rotation, replay refusal,
  reuse-detection lookup, family revocation, audience-scoped active listing, one-active
  OTP, single-consume races, atomic attempts, expiry, single-use tokens, invite
  acceptance, and the TTL policy including the deliberate invite exception. Gates:
  typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15, `pnpm test` 9/9, naming, format green. Next: D2 session
  lifecycle (issue/refresh/revoke + reuse detection wired to cookies, CSRF bound to
  `csrfSecretHash`, audience guards). Tool: claude-code.

No API/DB source was changed during portal reconciliation: Schema Nebula remains 64 nodes / 7 current / 57 target-only, and its compiler now excludes those four model files from target-star evidence until the adapter owner aligns the six target-backed names. Tool: codex. Notes: the source-only database catalogue still owns reporting every current Mongoose model in Pass 4.
- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk C Pass 2 (Mongo transaction manager + replica-set commit/rollback proof).**
  `MongoTransactionManager` implements `TransactionManagerPort` at the adapter edge:
  it opens a driver session, exposes it through the opaque domain
  `TransactionContext`, and uses `AsyncLocalStorage` so a NESTED `withTransaction`
  JOINS the outer session instead of opening a second transaction (which is what the
  port documents, so the contract is now true rather than aspirational). Errors
  propagate unchanged so the global filter can classify them; `maxRetries` is
  deliberately not forwarded because the driver's own transient/commit-unknown retry
  loop governs — noted in the code rather than silently ignored. `sessionFrom()` is
  the single extraction point for repositories. Bound in DI as `TRANSACTION_MANAGER`.
  New `test/transaction.test.ts` (6 tests, `RUN_DB_IT=1` against rs0): commit persists
  every write; a throw after a SUCCESSFUL write rolls that write back too; the original
  error type survives; the work's return value is returned; a nested call observes the
  same session object; and an inner failure rolls back the outer writes as well. These
  are real proof rather than a tautology — the commit case shows the same code path
  does persist, so the rollback case cannot be passing merely because nothing was
  written. Adapter tests 7 → **13**. Gates: typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15,
  `pnpm test` 9/9, naming, format green. Chunk C now has request-id/logging/errors/
  proxy/CORS/health (Pass 1) and the transaction profile (Pass 2); the cookie/CSRF
  foundation remains, and is where Chunk D picks up. Tool: claude-code.

- 2026-08-02 — [DONE] **Auth Pass 1 — physical collection names reconciled with the
  ratified Schema Nebula graph (closes the 2026-08-01 D1 BLOCKED bullet below).**
  The auth matrix's **RECONCILE FIRST** row was understated: `grep "collection:"` across
  `packages/adapters-db-mongo/src/models` returned **zero** matches, so _every_ model took
  the Mongoose default, not only the six auth ones. Live `getCollectionNames()` on rs0
  confirmed 24 divergent physical names — `authsessions`, `otpchallenges`, `oauthstates`,
  `passwordresettokens`, `emailverificationtokens`, `admininvites`, plus `auditlogs` and
  `consentevents` (both locked graph nodes), the whole Chunk-E catalog/media/inventory/
  content set, and two the default also mis-pluralized: `inventoryledgers` and
  `messageoutboxes` against the graph's singular `inventoryLedger` / `messageOutbox`.
  All **32** schemas now pass `collection: '<name>'` explicitly, including the seven that
  already matched — an implicit name is a name Mongoose may change for us during a future
  model rename. New `src/collection-names.ts` is the single declaration those literals are
  checked against, and records two things the list alone would blur: `authRateLimits` is
  deliberately **not** a graph node (auth architecture §7 keeps rate-limit backing an
  adapter/runtime concern), and `productQuestions` / `ratingAggregates` are implemented
  models the ratified 64-node graph carries **no node for** — a portal/owner documentation
  gap recorded rather than papered over by renaming working collections.
  **Migration, not "new collections will just appear".** Changing `collection` moves where
  the driver reads and writes; existing documents stay behind, invisible. For sessions that
  silently signs everyone out, and for `auditLogs` it orphans retained security evidence the
  retention lock does not permit dropping. `src/migrations/align-collection-names.ts` +
  `pnpm mongo:align-collections [-- --dry-run]` therefore moves the data in the same
  reviewed step: idempotent (a second run reports `already-aligned` and issues no command),
  **refuses to merge** when both names exist (reports `conflict`; guessing a winner could
  destroy audit rows), and omits `dropTarget` — the one flag that could delete data.
  Rollback is the inverse rename; no document is modified. Deliberately **not** run at boot
  (`AGENTS.md` + the index-lifecycle rule forbid uncontrolled startup schema mutation).
  **Verified on the real rs0 (`mongodb://127.0.0.1:27018/saha_textile_local?replicaSet=rs0&directConnection=true`):**
  dry run listed 24 pairs and changed nothing; the real run renamed **24/24, 0 conflicts**;
  an immediate second run reported **renamed=0**; `getCollectionNames()` now returns 32
  camelCase names with **no lowercase straggler**; and `getIndexes()` captured before and
  after on `authSessions`/`otpChallenges`/`adminInvites`/`auditLogs`/`mediaAssets` is
  **byte-identical** (rename carries indexes, so the catalogue the adapter suite proves
  survives). **Live API probe on a fresh build**, which is what proves the running system
  uses the new names rather than recreating the old ones: register → 201 with
  `st_access`/`st_refresh`/`st_csrf` cookies and **no token material in the body**; `/me`
  200; `/auth/admin/me` with a storefront cookie **401** (audience isolation); refresh
  without the CSRF header **403** and with it **200**, after which the rotated session still

  New `test/collection-names.test.ts` (8 tests) binds the model literals to the governed

## Auth & security

- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk C closed out (config/docs alignment).** Added `LOG_LEVEL`
  to `apps/api/.env.example` and the `environment-variables.mdx` catalogue so the new
  pino level is documented rather than an undeclared knob; noted there that secret
  headers/bodies are redacted at the logger regardless of level. Chunk C's Definition of
  Done is now met end to end: API boots, `/health/live` and `/health/ready` behave
  correctly (readiness proven to fail closed with Mongo stopped), transaction
  commit/rollback runs against a real replica set, and no Atlas/Brevo/`blr1`/browser-secret
  config remains. Portal-truth snapshot: chunk C `partial` → `done`. Remaining
  rate-limit work is per-route-class tuning for auth/OTP/checkout/search, which belongs
  with the Chunk D endpoints that need it. Tool: claude-code.
- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Chunk C Pass 3 (cookie + double-submit CSRF foundation).**
  Registered `@fastify/cookie` (unsigned — session integrity comes from the opaque
  token's entropy and its server-side hash, not a cookie signature). New
  `common/cookies.ts` centralizes attributes: session cookies are `httpOnly` +
  `SameSite=Lax` + `Path=/`, `Secure` only in production, and the **`__Host-` prefix
  is applied exactly when it is valid** — production AND no pinned `COOKIE_DOMAIN`,
  because the browser rejects `__Host-` if `Domain` is present or the connection is
  plain HTTP; a pinned domain therefore falls back to the compact `st_*` name (both
  spellings are owner-allowed). The CSRF cookie is deliberately NOT `httpOnly`:
  double-submit requires the app to read and echo it, and it authenticates nothing on
  its own. New `CsrfGuard` registered via `APP_GUARD` so routes are protected by
  DEFAULT rather than by remembering to opt in: safe methods pass; unsafe methods pass
  when no session cookie is present (nothing to forge, and bearer clients are immune
  by construction); otherwise the header must match the cookie under a constant-time
  compare, and the rejection message is generic so it does not tell an attacker which
  half was wrong. `GET /auth/csrf` issues a 32-byte CSPRNG token in the body and as the
  readable cookie, matching the `CsrfTokenResponse` contract. **Live probe on a fresh
  build:** `/auth/csrf` → 200 with a readable `st_csrf` cookie; POST with a session
  cookie and no header → **403**; wrong header → **403**; matching pair → **201**;
  no cookies at all → **201**; and the 403 body is the `ApiError` envelope
  (`forbidden`, generic message, requestId). 15 new unit tests (28 in `apps/api`).
  Gates: typecheck 13/13, lint 15/15, `pnpm test` 9/9, naming, format green.
  Chunk D binds the token to `authSessions.csrfSecretHash` so a token from one session
  cannot be replayed against another. Tool: claude-code.
- 2026-07-24 — [PARTIAL] Target = httpOnly cookies + CSRF + rotating refresh; current API still bearer-oriented. Phase/Chunk D. Tool: cursor.
- 2026-07-24 — [DONE] Admin PIN UX locked in owner log (2026-07-23). Tool: cursor.
- 2026-08-01 — [DONE] **Session sid binding + CSRF fail-closed + CSRF Policy B + cart/order BOLA.**
  Verified defects were real in source: access JWTs survived logout/family revoke until
  JWT expiry; CSRF passed when session lookup was null; `GET /auth/csrf` desynced active
  sessions; carts/orders accepted foreign cart ids; order create was non-atomic. Fixes
  and tests recorded under API + DB sections above. Tool: cursor.
- 2026-08-02 — [DONE] **Storefront/admin authentication use-case and security contract
  captured.** Added the verbatim approved touchpoint/status matrices plus ratified
  boundaries for shared `/auth/csrf`, storefront `/auth/storefront/**`, admin
  `/auth/admin/**`, admin-only RBAC, cookie-session security, provider credential gates,
  negative tests, and staged Claude Code delivery. Documentation only: fake-token Angular
  reconciliation, OAuth/MSG91 E2E, RBAC completion, and the remaining D3-D5 implementation
  evidence are still pending. Tool: codex.
- 2026-08-02 — [DONE] **Auth Pass 2 — T0 reconciliation: every fake/browser-stored token
  removed and both Angular apps put on the cookie session (closes the matrix's two
  `RECONCILE FIRST` client rows, one of them `SECURITY CRITICAL`).**
  **What was actually wrong.** The storefront seeded a constant `FAKE_ACCESS_TOKEN` in the
  store's `onInit`; the admin seeded `FAKE_TOKEN` **and persisted its whole state to
  `localStorage` under `auth_store`**. Both forwarded it as `Authorization: Bearer`, and
  both login forms shipped working demo credentials (`admin@example.com`/`123456789`,
  `john.customer@example.com`/`123456789`) and navigated to the dashboard **before** any
  API call. Worth stating precisely: the fake token granted **nothing** — `SessionGuard`
  reads the access cookie only and the API ignores bearer entirely — so this was misleading
  state and a wrong-shaped client, not an exploitable hole. It still had to go first,
  because `isAuthenticated` was true before anyone signed in, which would have hidden any
  real authorization defect behind it.
  **Boundary now in place, one per app.** `core/auth/auth-gateway.ts` is an abstract class
  used as the DI token; `core/auth/http-auth.gateway.ts` is the ONLY file in each app
  holding auth route paths and wire DTOs. Components, guards and stores depend on the
  gateway, never on `HttpClient`. No prototype mutation, no inheritance-heavy base service.
  The stores became session facades holding **sanitized identity plus a three-valued status**
  (`unknown`/`anonymous`/`authenticated`) — the third value matters: collapsing "not resolved
  yet" into "anonymous" bounces a signed-in user to login during hydration.
  `provideSessionBootstrap()` resolves `/me` before routing and awaits a new
  `runtimeConfigReady` promise, because Angular starts app initializers CONCURRENTLY and the
  gateway needs the real `apiUrl` — assuming registration order would have been a race. On
  the storefront it is browser-only: SSR has no cookie jar, and rendering an account shell
  server-side is what the no-per-user-SSR-cache rule forbids.
  **Screens wired to real endpoints** (storefront): password login, registration, email-OTP
  login, forgot-password, token-based reset. Two template flows were reshaped rather than
  wired as found — the template chained forgot-password → OTP → update-password, conflating
  a single-use emailed reset TOKEN with a purpose-bound OTP CHALLENGE. Recovery now
  acknowledges and stops (generic, so it cannot be used to test whether an address exists),
  `update-password` redeems `?token=` from the emailed link, and the OTP screen became what
  the API actually offers: email-OTP **login**.
  **Admin screens deleted, not stubbed.** `auth/otp`, `auth/forgot-password` and
  `auth/update-password` are gone with their routes. The OTP screen is `DO NOT BUILD AS
LOGIN` — leaving a reachable route that mints a session from an emailed code contradicts
  the locked admin method list, and "it doesn't work yet" is not a control. The other two
  implemented the template's OTP-token recovery against `/auth/admin/password/**`, which
  **does not exist**; a recovery screen that cannot recover is worse than none because it
  reads as supported. They return with the endpoints, in the canonical shape. Admin login
  now offers the two locked methods (identifier + password, identifier + six-digit PIN) with
  the identifier validated as a string, not an email, since usernames are valid.
  **Mechanical guard added.** `scripts/check-browser-auth.mjs`, wired into `pnpm lint`
  beside the naming law, enforces five rules across both apps: no fake/demo token, no bearer
  header, no session in `localStorage`/`sessionStorage`/IndexedDB, auth paths only in the
  one gateway, and **no cross-audience route** (storefront code cannot name `/auth/admin`,
  and the reverse). It strips comments before matching — a checker that forbids documenting
  the rule it enforces is a checker people delete. **Mutation-verified:** a probe file
  containing all five violations produced 8 errors across all 5 rules; removing it went
  green. A unit test could not host this (it needs Node's fs, and neither browser app has or
  should have Node types).
  **i18n:** failure states are Transloco KEYS, not English sentences in TypeScript — the
  Machine-1 rule — and one generic key covers every credential failure so the UI cannot
  re-leak the account existence the API's uniform 401 hides. 7 keys added to each app's
  `en.json` by textual append (a `JSON.parse`/`stringify` round-trip reordered keys and
  retabbed the file; that was reverted). French deliberately NOT authored — `DEC-TRANSLATION-QUALITY`
  is unresolved and `fallbackLang: 'en'` renders English rather than a placeholder token.
  **Two real defects found while testing.** (1) `logout()` used `try/finally`, which does
  not swallow — the store cleared correctly but the promise still rejected into click
  handlers; now caught explicitly, and three call sites `void` it. (2) The admin PIN-lock
  message is **unreachable**: `AdminAuthController` throws "PIN is temporarily locked; sign
  in with your password", but `HttpExceptionFilter` replaces every 401 with the generic
  `unauthorized` envelope, so nothing distinguishing crosses the boundary. Client-side
  detection was written, found dead, and removed rather than left as decoration; the login
  screen shows a standing hint instead. **The API needs a stable machine-readable sub-code
  for this — tracked for the admin pass.**
  **Live browser verification (storefront, real dev server + real API on a fresh build).**
  Login page renders with **empty** credential fields. Registered a real customer through
  the UI: landed on `/account/dashboard`; `localStorage` held only `ngrx_cart`, `language`,
  `site_config_session`; `sessionStorage` empty; `indexedDB.databases()` empty; and the
  **only readable cookie was `st_csrf`** — `st_access`/`st_refresh` invisible to
  `document.cookie`, which is the httpOnly proof. **Full page reload kept the session and
  the protected route**, proving it is re-read from cookies via `/me` rather than restored
  from storage. Logout → `/auth/login`; re-requesting `/account/dashboard` afterwards
  redirected to login. Network trace, in order: `/me` **401** → `/auth/csrf` 200 → register
  **201** (CORS preflight 204) → `/auth/csrf` 200 → after reload `/me` **200** → logout
  **204** → `/me` **401**. The logout 204 is itself evidence the CSRF header injection works:
  an unsafe cookie-authenticated request without a matching header is a 403.
  **Admin browser E2E NOT run — stated plainly.** The Browser pane became unavailable
  mid-session and would not composite frames; retries and a fresh tab did not recover it.
  Admin is covered by unit tests, the lint guard, and a **built-bundle scan** instead, and
  its in-browser run is owed. Independently, there is **no first-administrator bootstrap
  anywhere in the repo**, so no admin account can exist yet to sign in with — that is Pass 6.
  **Built-bundle scan (the matrix's explicit requirement), 74 admin + 416 storefront JS
  files:** `laravel_sanctum`, `FAKE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `FAKE_TOKEN`, `admin@example.com`,
  `john.customer@example.com`, `Bearer ${` and `auth_store` are **all absent from both**;
  the three deleted admin routes are absent; `/auth/admin/` appears in exactly one chunk. The
  scan is not vacuous — the same patterns are still present in `git show HEAD` for those
  files (4 matches in the admin store, 2 in the storefront store, 2 demo credentials in the
  admin login).
  **Tests:** storefront 0 → **8**, admin 0 → **9** (the first specs either app has had since
  the scaffold spec was removed on 2026-07-31). Each asserts something the previous
  implementation would fail, including "writes nothing to browser storage across a full
  session lifecycle". **Mutation-verified:** flipping the admin store's initial status to
  `authenticated` fails `starts unresolved rather than authenticated`; restored, green.
  Gates: `pnpm typecheck` 13/13, `pnpm lint` 15/15 (0 errors) + `check:naming` +
  `check:browser-auth`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm turbo run test --force` **9/9**
  (contracts 140, core 36, api 64, admin 9, storefront 8), `MONGODB_PORT=27018 RUN_DB_IT=1`
  adapter IT **89/89**, both Angular production builds, `git diff --check` clean. Probe user
  and its session/token/counter rows deleted afterwards (`users` back to 0); API log scan
  found no credential values, only route-mapping lines. Tool: claude-code.
  **Known gaps this pass deliberately did NOT close:** refresh single-flight (concurrent
  401s each rotate — `TODO(auth pass 3)` in both interceptors); the shared transport package
  (the CSRF cookie reader is duplicated per app, and neither Angular app consumes
  `@saha-textile/contracts` at all yet); protected deep-link continuation beyond the
  storefront's existing `redirectUrl`; admin idle soft lock and quick-resume UX; and the
  storefront's global error interceptor logging expected anonymous `/me` 401s as errors.
  **Next executable step:** Pass 3 — the shared transport primitive as a workspace package,
  contracts consumption in both apps, and refresh single-flight.
- 2026-08-02 — [REVERTED] **Pass 2's deletion of the admin `forgot-password` and
  `update-password` screens was wrong and has been undone.** The matrix marks
  `Admin password recovery request` **GOOD TO GO — LOCAL SUBSTITUTE** and
  `Admin password reset` **GOOD TO GO** — instructions to build, not to remove. Deleting
  their UI exceeded what was approved; the reasoning behind it (a route with no endpoint
  reads as supported when it is not) held only for the OTP screen, whose row genuinely does
  say `DO NOT BUILD AS LOGIN`. Screens restored from git and rebuilt against real endpoints;
  see the entry below. Owner raised this directly. Tool: claude-code.
- 2026-08-02 — [DONE] **Admin password recovery built end to end (Auth Passes 2a–2f).**
  Delivered in six small passes so each stayed independently verifiable.
  **2a — contracts.** `AdminPasswordForgotRequest` (identifier only) and
  `AdminPasswordResetRequest` (token + 12-char password floor). One test asserts the forgot
  shape has EXACTLY one key, which is structural proof it cannot quietly grow an OTP code
  field later. Contracts 140 → **145**.
  **2b — `POST /auth/admin/password/forgot`.** Public (an operator who cannot sign in has no
  session), rate-limited by identifier AND source IP, accepts email or username like admin
  login. `startAdminPasswordReset` refuses unknown identifiers, customer accounts, disabled
  accounts and admins with no email address — all silently, because the controller answers
  generically either way. **Live-probed:** an unknown identifier and a real admin looked up
  **by username** returned **byte-identical** bodies (`cmp` clean); malformed → 400. The DB
  showed one `passwordResetTokens` row (`audience: admin`, 64-char hash, no plaintext field),
  **0 OTP challenges** and **0 sessions** — the last two being the real proof this is
  recovery and not a login path. API 64 → **71**.
  **2c — `POST /auth/admin/password/reset` + the locked security behavior.** New
  `users.pinRevalidationRequiredAt` threaded contract → model → port → adapter, kept separate
  from `pinLockedUntil` on purpose: that one is the five-failure brute-force lock and
  self-heals after fifteen minutes, while this one has no expiry and clears only on a
  successful password login. Collapsing them would let a timer silently restore a credential
  a suspected compromise had suspended. `recordSuccessfulLogin` clears it in the SAME write,
  so no caller can skip it. The reset is audience-bound. **Live-probed against a real admin
  account:** a storefront-audience token against the admin reset → **401** with the password
  unchanged and sessions intact, yet the token still consumed so it cannot be retried on the
  storefront; the admin-audience token → **204**; replay → **401**; after the reset the
  password changed, **the PIN hash was KEPT not deleted**, revalidation was set,
  `tokenVersion` went 0 → 1, live admin sessions **0** and revoked **1**; PIN login while
  suspended → **401**; password login with the new password → **200** and the suspension
  cleared with the PIN hash still present. A bonus finding: inserting a second reset row with
  an identical hash failed `E11000 tokenHash_1`, confirming the unique index works. Log scan
  found no token or password material. API 71 → **79**.
  **2d — admin client.** Gateway gained `requestPasswordReset`/`resetPassword`; the HTTP
  adapter gained both paths and remains the only file in the app holding them. A successful
  reset clears local session state because the server has already revoked everything. Admin
  tests 9 → **12**; 8 i18n keys appended textually so file order and tabs survived.
  **2e — screens restored and rewired.** `forgot-password` → generic acknowledgement with no
  branch on existence; `update-password` reads `?token=` so the emailed link and the paste
  path converge; `otp` **repurposed** as the recovery-token step. The repurposing is
  structural rather than documented: `otp.ts` does not inject `AuthStore` at all — the only
  match for that name in the file is the comment explaining why — so it has no route to a
  login, OTP-verify or session method. Its field is a free-form token, not a six-digit box,
  so nobody is invited to wire it to a challenge. **Bundle-verified:** all three routes and
  both recovery endpoints present, and **zero** `email-otp` references anywhere in the admin
  bundle.
  **2f — KB.** New matrix §2.5 records the disposition and the correction. §1 was left
  untouched: it is the approved review preserved word for word, so an implementation
  reconciliation belongs in the addendum, not in those tables.
  Gates after each pass: `pnpm typecheck` 13/13, `pnpm lint` 15/15 (0 errors) +
  `check:naming` + `check:browser-auth`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm turbo run test --force`
  **9/9**, adapter IT **89/89**, admin production build. Probe admin and all its rows deleted
  afterwards (`users` back to 0). Tool: claude-code.
  **Known gaps.** The API still cannot tell a client WHY a PIN was refused — the controller
  raises a specific message but the global filter replaces every 401 with the generic
  `unauthorized` envelope, so the UI shows a standing hint instead of a precise reason; a
  stable machine-readable sub-code is needed. No first-administrator bootstrap exists, so
  recovery was probed against a directly-seeded account rather than an invited one. Admin
  browser E2E is still owed from Pass 2. Real email delivery remains the console adapter.
  Developer-portal derived pages were deliberately NOT touched in these passes at the owner's
  instruction; a separate tool reconciles them, and the portal validator will flag the
  affected pages until it runs.
  **Next executable step:** Pass 3 — shared transport primitive as a workspace package,
  contracts consumption in both Angular apps, and refresh single-flight.
- 2026-08-02 — [DONE] **Auth Pass 3a — `@saha-textile/http-transport`, the shared transport
  primitive, created and proven to integrate. Package only; no app consumes it yet (3b).**
  **Why alone.** Neither Angular app depended on ANY workspace package before this
  (verified: only `apps/api` had `workspace:*` entries), so the risk being isolated was not
  the logic — it was whether a new package resolves, compiles and bundles through Analog/Vite
  SSR and the admin builder under its `strict: false` tsconfig. Bundling that question with a
  behaviour change would have made a failure ambiguous.
  **What it owns** (auth matrix §2.2, minus what would have been dead code): the
  double-submit CSRF contract (`CSRF_HEADER_NAME`, both accepted cookie spellings, a pure
  `readCsrfTokenFrom(jar)` plus an SSR-safe ambient reader), `RefreshCoordinator`
  single-flight, safe error mapping (`toTransportFailure` + `isUnauthorized`/`isForbidden`/
  `isRetryable`), base-URL joining with an absolute/protocol-relative refusal, and request-id
  reading. It owns NO business policy — no route paths, no audience, no store — because those
  differ per app and a shared module holding them becomes the god service §2.2 forbids.
  **Cancellation was deliberately NOT abstracted:** Angular cancels by unsubscribing, and a
  framework-free `AbortSignal` wrapper nothing calls is exactly the dead client code the
  continuation brief's rule 8 warns about. It stays in each app's HTTP adapter.
  **Three decisions worth stating.** (1) **ESM output** (`"type": "module"`), unlike
  `contracts`/`core-domain`, because both apps bundle this directly and a CJS workspace
  dependency is the classic source of optimization-bailout warnings. (2) **No `DOM` lib** —
  ambient `document` is reached through a narrow `globalThis` declaration, so every export is
  unit-testable in Node and safe to evaluate under SSR rather than throwing. (3) **No runtime
  dependency on `@saha-textile/contracts`.** The error envelope is declared structurally
  here; a value import would pull Zod into both browser bundles as a side effect of adopting
  the transport, pre-empting the bundle-size question pass 3d is supposed to decide
  deliberately. `pnpm install` added/downloaded **0** packages — the package has zero
  third-party dependencies.
  **Guards, all mutation-verified.** A root ESLint block mirrors the core-domain rule:
  contracts is `allowTypeImports`, Zod/Angular/RxJS/Analog/NestJS/Fastify/Mongoose/adapters
  are refused outright. `test/runtime-purity.test.ts` compiles to a throwaway dir and asserts
  the emitted JS imports **nothing at all** — not even a `node:` builtin, which is where it
  differs from core's. `test/contract-alignment.test.ts` asserts the duplicated error shape
  stays assignable to the contract **in both directions** and enumerates the code union as a
  `Record<ContractApiErrorCode, true>`, so a code added to contracts and forgotten here fails
  to compile. **Proofs run:** a value import of contracts appended to `errors.ts` produced 1
  ESLint error AND 2 purity-test failures (`errors.js references/imports
@saha-textile/contracts`); deleting `'conflict'` from the local union failed typecheck on
  the alignment assertions naming exactly that member. Both probes reverted, green.
  **Two real defects found and fixed while building.** (1) `*/` inside two doc comments
  (`/;\s*/` and `POST /auth/*/refresh`) terminated the comment early and produced 14 parse
  errors — the build caught it, not review. (2) The purity scanner matched the package's own
  doc comments explaining why contracts/`document` are absent; it now strips comments first,
